   The Typikon is here a little below, following the enumeration of all 14 cases.
8. On the Memorial Saturday before Pentecost Sunday.  
   It is transferred to the preceding Thursday.
9. On Pentecost Sunday.  
   It is transferred to the preceding Thursday.
10. On Monday of Pentecost.  
   It is transferred to the following Tuesday.

11. On Saturday before All Saints, with the Apodosis of the Descent of the Holy Spirit.  
   The Typikon is here a little below, following the enumeration of all 14 cases.
12. On the Sunday of All Saints.  
   It is transferred back to Friday.
13. On Thursday of the Eucharist.  
   According to the general Typikon of a Saint with Polyeleos within a Feast.
14. On the Sunday of the Eucharist (only the Finding).  
   It is transferred back to Saturday, or forward to Monday.

### Typikon for Cases 7 and 11: Constantine and Helen, and the Finding of the Head of the Forerunner at the Apodosis of the Ascension and of Pentecost

#### At Great Vespers
1. **"Blessed is the man"** (*Блаженъ мужъ*).
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), 10 Stichera: 6 of the Feast, and 4 of the Saint; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Feast.
3. Prokimenon of the day, and 3 Readings of the Saint.
4. Aposticha of the Feast; only the Doxastikon of the Saint.
5. Troparion of the Saint; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast.

#### At Great Matins
1. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Богъ Господь*), Troparion of the Feast twice; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Feast.
2. The first two Sessional Hymns of the Feast; after the Polyeleos with the Magnifications, two Sessional Hymns of the Saint, then **"Glory..."**, his Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; **"Both now..."**, of the Feast. All the rest up to the Canon is of the Saint.
3. 3 Canons for 14: the first of the Feast with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Saint for 4, and the second of the Feast for 4. Katavasia

of the day. After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion, Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the Saint; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast; at the 9th Ode we do not sing **"More honorable than the Cherubim"** (*Честнѣйшую*), but the Refrain of the Feast; and to the Troparia of the Saint we chant his usual Refrain; after the 9th Ode, Exaposteilarion of the Feast; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Feast.
4. At the Praises, 6 Stichera: 3 of the Feast, and 3 of the Saint, with his two verses; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Feast.
5. After the Great Doxology, Troparion of the Saint; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast.

#### At the Hours
At all [the Hours], Troparion of the Feast; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; Kontakion, at the 1st and 6th, of the Feast; at the 3rd and 9th, of the Saint.

#### At the Divine Liturgy
1. Troparion of the Feast and of the Saint; **"Glory..."**, Kontakion of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Feast.
2. Prokimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Hymn of the Feast and of the Saint; Epistle and Gospel of the day and of the Saint.

### June

### June 11: The Holy Apostles Bartholomew and Barnabas
They have the following 11 cases:
1. On one of the weekdays of Pentecost.  
   According to the Typikon of George here on p. 324.

2. On Friday of the Apodosis of the Ascension.  
   According to the Typikon of Constantine and Helen here on p. 340.
3. On Memorial Saturday of Pentecost.  
   It is transferred to the preceding Thursday.
4. On Pentecost Sunday.  
   It is transferred to the preceding Thursday.
5. On Monday of Pentecost.  
   It is transferred to the following Tuesday.
6. On Saturday of the Apodosis of Pentecost.  
   According to the Typikon of Constantine and Helen here on p. 340.
7. On the Sunday of All Saints.  
   It is transferred back to Friday.
8. On Thursday of the Eucharist.  
   According to the general Typikon of a Saint with Polyeleos within a Feast.
9. On the Sunday of the Eucharist.  
   It is transferred back to Saturday, or forward to Monday.
10. At the Apodosis of the Feast of the Eucharist.  
   According to the Typikon of Constantine and of the Finding, when they occur at the Apodosis of the Ascension and of Pentecost, here on p. 340.
11. On the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos.  
   According to the Typikon of the Theologian, when it occurs on the Ascension, here on p. 333[^445]; however, without a Vigil.

### June 24: The Nativity of the Holy Forerunner and Baptist John
### and June 29: The Holy Preeminent Apostles Peter and Paul

These two Feasts, as also the Beheading of the Precious Head of the Forerunner, have the general Typikon of a Saint with Vigil; nevertheless they surpass a Saint with Vigil in the following points:
1. At Matins, before their Canon, the Canon of the Theotokos is not taken, but only their two Canons for 14: the Heirmos of the 1st Canon twice, then the Troparia of both Canons for 12, that is, 6 of the one, and 6 of the other.
2. At the Divine Liturgy, the Epistle and Gospel are read only of the Feast; but that of the day is read a day earlier, as on the Dominical and Marian Feasts.
3. Their Service is not celebrated on a single day, but on two; nevertheless on the second day their Service is of daily rank, and joined with the Service of the Octoechos and of the Saint of the day, so that the Octoechos occupies the first place, the Forerunner or the Preeminent Apostles the second, according to the Typikon of a Saint for 4, and the Saint of the day the third. It is fitting also to take their Troparion and Kontakion of the second day likewise at the Hours and at the Divine Liturgy.

### Typikon for June 24 and June 29 if It Occurs with the Feast of the Eucharist and the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos[^446]

With respect to the Feast of the Eucharist, these Saints have three cases:
1. Within the Feast of the Eucharist.
2. On the Sunday unto which the Feast of the Eucharist is transferred.
3. At the Apodosis of the Feast of the Eucharist.

For the 1st case, that is, within the Feast, the Order is according to the general Typikon of a Saint with Vigil within a Feast, as also on the Thursday of the Feast itself, for the Service of the Feast with the All-Night Vigil is transferred to Sunday.

For the 2nd case, that is, on the Sunday of the Eucharist, by decree of the Synod of Lviv, the Resurrectional Service is omitted, and according to our opinion (for there is no Typikon) the Service of the Feast is to be sung with the Service of the Forerunner or of the Preeminent Apostles, according to the Typikon of the Theologian of May 8, when it occurs on the Ascension, here on p. 333.

For the 3rd case, that is, at the Apodosis of the Eucharist, the Order is according to the Typikon of the Finding of the Head of the Forerunner, May 25, when this occurs at the Apodosis of the Ascension or of the Descent of the Holy Spirit, here on p. 340. Nevertheless, since here there is an All-Night Vigil, which the Finding does not have, on this

account here at Small Vespers after the Stichera at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*) and at the Aposticha is sung **"Both now..."**, the Sticheron of the Feast; after the Troparion of the Saints: **"Glory... Both now..."**, Troparion of the Feast; likewise also at the Litiya, the Stichera of the Saints with **"Both now..."** of the Feast; at the Blessing of Loaves, the Troparion of the Saints twice, and **"O Theotokos and Virgin"** (*Богородице Дѣво*) once[^447].

With respect to the Feast of the Compassion, with which these Saints can collide, according to our opinion (for the Typikon contains nothing), the Order is to be according to the Typikon of the Theologian of May 8, when he occurs on the Feast of the Ascension, here on p. 333. Nevertheless, since the Compassion does not have an All-Night Vigil, on this account at Small Vespers all is sung only of the Saints; at Great Matins all the Sessional Hymns will be of the Saints; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast; all the rest up to the Canon is only of the Saints; the Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Saints for 8. If June 25 and 30, that is, the second day of the Feast of these Saints, occurs with the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos, then their Service is taken at Compline.

### July

### July 2: Deposition of the Precious Robe of the Most Holy Theotokos
Polyeleos optional[^448].

#### 1. On Weekdays
If this Service is not sung with the Polyeleos, then it has the daily form, with Stichera at the Praises and the Great Doxology; nevertheless on weekdays it excludes the Service of the Octoechos; but if with the Polyeleos, then it has the form of Great Vespers and Great Matins.

#### 2. On Sunday
For the case of Sunday the Church books provide no Typikon. We deem, however, that at Vespers, if the Polyeleos is not sung, at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*) there will be 6 Resurrectional Stichera, and 4 of the Theotokos[^449]; but if the Polyeleos, then 4 Resurrectional, and 6 of the Theotokos; **"Glory..."**, of the Theotokos; **"Both now..."**, the Dogmatikon of the Tone; and at the Aposticha, **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Robe. The Troparia at the conclusion of Vespers, and at **"God is the Lord"** (*Богъ Господь*), we conclude with the Troparion of the Robe, and at the Divine Liturgy with the Kontakion of the Robe.  
At Compline after **"It is truly right"** (*Достойно есть*), Kontakion of the Robe.  
At Great Matins, if there is a Polyeleos, then from the Gradual Hymns up to the Canon all is sung only Resurrectional as usual; all the rest at Matins, at the Hours, and at the Divine Liturgy is sung according to the general Typikon of a Marian Feast on Sunday. But if there is no Polyeleos, then we do not sing the Sessional Hymns of the Robe, nor the Polyeleos and the Magnifications; all the rest we sing according to the general Typikon of a Marian Feast.

### Typikon of the Deposition of the Precious Robe on the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos[^450]
In the Church books there is no Typikon for this case; nevertheless we deem that it is fitting then to transfer the Service

of the Deposition to the following Saturday, and to chant the Service of the Saint of the day of that Saturday at Compline.

### July 16: The Holy Fathers of the First Six Ecumenical Councils
All is according to the Typikon of the Holy Fathers of the Seventh Ecumenical Council, October 11, here on p. 145.

### July 20: The Holy Prophet Elijah
The Service of the Prophet by decree of the Synod of Lviv is to be celebrated with an All-Night Vigil, and from a weekday transferred to Sunday.  
All is according to the general Typikon of a Saint with Vigil occurring on Sunday[^451].

> **Note:** If the Sunday unto which the Service of the Prophet is transferred occurs on July 25, where there is a Polyeleos, then it is fitting to transfer it to the preceding Sunday.

### July 25: Dormition of St. Anne, Mother of the Most Holy Theotokos
And the Commemoration of the Holy Women Olympias and Eupraxia.

> **Note:** Although in the ancient Typika the Service of St. Anne is appointed of lesser rank, with the Service of the Octoechos and of the Holy Women[^452], nevertheless with time it was deemed worthy of a Polyeleos among us, and by choice and decree of the Synod of Zamost, even of an All-Night Vigil[^453] (although this does not take place among us, but only a Polyeleos); among the Greeks, however, it has Great Vespers with Stichera at the Litiya, and likewise Great Matins from the Canon downwards[^454].  
> "We chant the Canon of the Holy Women at Compline, when the Superior wills"[^455].

### Typikon
All is according to the general Rule of a Saint with Polyeleos, whether on Sunday or on weekdays; nevertheless we do not conclude the Stichera and Troparia with the Theotokia of the Octoechos, but with those of the Saint, as though Marian, only on Sunday at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*) with the Dogmatikon of the Tone, and at the Praises with the Marian **"Most blessed art Thou"** (*Преблагословенна*).

### July 31: Forefeast of the Procession of the Precious Cross[^456]
And the Holy and Righteous Eudocimus.

> **Notes:**  
> 1. The Feast of the Procession of the Precious Wood of August 1 has a Forefeast, which is today, that is, July 31, but it has no Afterfeast.  
> 2. Although it has a Forefeast, nevertheless it is of lesser rank, that is, without a Polyeleos, and the Service of the Cross does not exclude the Service of the Saint of the day, nor of the Octoechos, neither on the Forefeast, nor on the Feast, neither on a Sunday, nor on a weekday.  
> 3. In the Forefeast and in the Feast, the first place is occupied by the Service of the Octoechos, the second by that of the Cross, the third by that of the Saint.  
> 4. The Service of the Forefeast has the composition of the Service of a Saint for 4, and the Service of St. Eudocimus is likewise of a Saint for 4; and since these two Services are joined to the Service of the Octoechos both on weekdays and on Sunday, the Order on weekdays is according to the general Typikon of two Saints occurring with the Octoechos on weekdays, only we conclude the Stichera at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*) and at the Aposticha with the Sticheron of the Forefeast, except on Friday evening, for then we conclude the Stichera at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*) with the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. On Sunday, however, the Order is according to the general Typikon of a Forefeast with a single Saint without Polyeleos with the Octoechos on Sunday. Likewise also we do not conclude the Troparia with the usual Theotokion, but with the Troparion of the Forefeast according to the general Typikon of a Forefeast. There will be, therefore:

### Typikon of the Forefeast of the Procession of the Precious Cross

#### 1. On Weekdays

##### At Vespers
1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), 6 Stichera: 3 of the Cross, and 3 of the Saint; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Cross. (On Friday evening, **"Glory..."**, of the Cross; **"Both now..."**, the Dogmatikon of the Tone[^457]).
2. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Octoechos; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Cross.
3. At the conclusion: Troparion of the Saint; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Cross.

##### At Compline
After **"It is truly right"** (*Достойно есть*), the Kontakion of the Cross alone.

##### At Matins
1. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Богъ Господь*), Troparion of the Cross twice; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Cross.
2. Both Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos.
3. 3 Canons for 14: one of the Octoechos for 6, and of the Cross for 4, and of the Saint for 4. Katavasia after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th Ode, the Heirmos of the last Canon. After the 3rd Ode, Sessional Hymn of the Saint; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Forefeast[^458]; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Saint; after the 9th Ode, Exaposteilarion of the Octoechos; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Cross.
4. Aposticha of the Octoechos; **"Glory..."**, of the Saint; **"Both now..."**, of the Cross.
5. At the conclusion, Troparion of the Saint; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Cross.

##### At the Hours and at the Divine Liturgy
All is according to the general Typikon of a Forefeast with a Saint without Polyeleos.

#### 2. On Sunday
All is according to the general Typikon of a Forefeast with a Saint without Polyeleos on Sunday, here on p. 88; only at the Praises, all the Stichera of the Octoechos, without the Forefeast.

### August

### August 1: Procession of the Precious Wood of the Precious Cross
And the Commemoration of the Holy Seven Maccabean Martyrs.

> **Note:** The Procession of the Precious Cross[^459], which formerly took place in Constantinople from the Imperial Palace unto the magnificent Temple of Sophia, we represent today by the bringing forth of the Precious Cross on the eve, first from the Skevophylakion unto the Holy Table, and then, on the very day of August 1, from the Holy Table unto the Tetrapod at Matins during the Doxology, where also the Veneration takes place, according to the Typikon of September 14 on p. 134, except for the Exaltation, which takes place only on September 14 on the Exaltation of the Precious Cross, but is omitted here. Besides this, in the Slavonic Typika there is appointed on this day also the Lesser Blessing of Water. Either at Matins directly before

the Veneration, or afterwards at another hour of the day, likewise in places also the blessing of herbs, as also on the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos[^460].

### Typikon

#### 1. On Weekdays
All is as on the Forefeast of July 31 here on p. 349; however:
1. After the Dismissal of Vespers occurs the bringing forth of the Precious Cross from the Skevophylakion unto the Holy Table, according to the Typikon of September 14 here on p. 135.
2. At Matins, Canon of the Octoechos for 4, and of the Cross for 6, and of the Saints for 4. Katavasia after each Ode of the day: **"A cross did Moses trace"** (*Крестъ начертавъ*) (from September 14). After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion, Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the Saints, only **"Both now..."**, of the Cross; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Cross; after the 9th Ode, Exaposteilarion of the Octoechos; **"Glory..."**, of the Saints; **"Both now..."**, of the Cross.
3. At the Praises, 6 Stichera: 3 of the Cross, and 3 of the Saints; **"Glory..."**, of the Saints; **"Both now..."**, of the Cross.
4. During the chanting of the Stichera of the Praises, the Priest vests in all the sacred vestments (of purple colour[^461]), and having exclaimed before the steps of the Holy Table: **"Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light"** (*Слава тебѣ показавшему намъ свѣтъ*), and censed the Precious Cross, brings it forth around the Holy Table through the northern

doors before the Holy Doors; and after the conclusion of the Great Doxology with the Trisagion, having exclaimed: **"Wisdom! Stand aright!"** (*Премудрость прости*), goes before the Tetrapod, and having placed the Precious Cross upon it, while the Choir sings the Troparion **"Save, O Lord, Thy people"** (*Спаси Господи*), censes it from the four sides, and makes the veneration of the Precious Cross, in everything as was set forth for September 14 here on p. 135.
5. The Blessing of Water, if it takes place outside of Matins, is performed in full, as you have in the Trebnik; but if at Matins after the singing of the Doxology and the Troparion **"Save, O Lord, Thy people"** (*Спаси Господи*), that is, directly before the veneration, then the beginning and the end of the Order of the Blessing of Water are omitted: for immediately after the Troparion **"Save, O Lord, Thy people"** (*Спаси Господи*), it begins from **"Rejoice"** (*Еже радуйся*), and concludes with the Sticheron **"O Lady, receive"** (*Владычице прійми*), omitting the Ektenia **"Have mercy on us, O God"** (*Помилуй насъ Боже*) with the rest.
6. The Blessing of Herbs, if it takes place anywhere, is usually performed at the Divine Liturgy after the Prayer behind the Ambon, invariably as on the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos.

#### 2. On Sunday
All is according to the general Typikon of an Afterfeast with a Saint without Polyeleos on Sunday, here on p. 50, only:
1. On Saturday evening after the Dismissal of Vespers occurs the bringing forth of the Precious Cross from the Skevophylakion unto the Holy Table; at Matins, during the Great Doxology, from the Holy Table unto the Tetrapod, according to the Typikon of September 14; however, after the exclamation **"Wisdom! Stand aright!"** (*Премудрость прости*), in place of the Troparion **"Save, O Lord, Thy people"** (*Спаси Господи*), we sing one of the two usual Resurrectional Troparia, during which it is fitting for the Priest to cense the Precious Cross from the four sides, and after this immediately to make the veneration of the Precious Cross, while singing the Troparion **"Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master"** (*Кресту твоему*

*покланяемся*); or before the veneration to take up also the Order of the Blessing of Water, according to the Typikon set down here for weekdays in item 5.
2. At the Divine Liturgy: The entire Service is taken first of Sunday, then of the Cross; but of the Saints, since they do not have a Polyeleos, we usually omit; but if anyone wills to take this also, then the Prokimena, Alleluias, and Communion Hymns will be only two: of the Cross and of the Saints; the Epistles and Gospels, however, three: of the day, of the Cross, and of the Saints[^462].

### August 6: The Transfiguration of Our Lord

All is according to the general Typikon of a Dominical Feast; only after the Prayer behind the Ambon[^463], as the Slavonic Typika have, or after the Dismissal of the Divine Liturgy, as the Trebnik of Peremyshl has, the Priest blesses on the Tetrapod the first-fruits of grapes (among us, however, since there are no grapes, the first-fruits of apples and other fruits) with the single prayer set down in the Menaion, or with the three prayers set down in our Trebnik[^464], with censing and the sprinkling of the same with holy water at the conclusion.

> **Note:** If this blessing takes place after the Prayer behind the Ambon, we deem that there is no need to recite beforehand the Usual Beginning and the Trisagion and the rest with the **"Our Father"** (*Отче нашъ*), and Psalm 66, and the Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast[^465],

but immediately **"Let us pray to the Lord"** (*Господу помолимся*), and the Prayer; for the Menaion indicates only the Prayer.

### August 15: The Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos

All is according to the general Typikon of Marian Feasts; only, if it is the custom anywhere, then after the Prayer behind the Ambon, or after the Dismissal of the Divine Liturgy, occurs the blessing of herbs, according to the Typikon set forth above for the blessing of fruits on August 6 here preceding.

### August 16: The Translation of the Image Not Made with Hands
And the Holy Martyr Diomedes.

> **Notes:**  
> 1. The Service of the Holy Martyr is sung at Compline, and two Services remain, that is, of the Afterfeast of the Dormition, and of the Image.  
> 2. Although the Service of the Image, as Dominical, is greater than that of the Theotokos, nevertheless here the Marian occupies the first place.  
> 3. The Service of the Image, according to the Typikon of our Menaion and the Russian ones, is for 6, and is celebrated according to the general Typikon of a Saint for 6 without Polyeleos, with Stichera at the Praises and the Great Doxology; it has also in the Canon the Katavasia of the day after each Ode, as also in the Slavonic books[^466]. The Dismissal is proper, expanded[^467].

4. Although the Service of the Image is not with Polyeleos, nevertheless at the Divine Liturgy we do not omit it, as neither the other Feasts without Polyeleos, Dominical and Marian.

### Typikon

#### 1. On Weekdays

##### At Vespers
1. There is no Kathisma, on account of the labor of the Vigil that took place.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), 6 Stichera: 3 of the Dormition, and 3 of the Image; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition. (On Friday evening, Theotokion of the Tone).
3. Aposticha of the Dormition; only the Doxastikon of the Image.
4. At the conclusion: Troparion of the Image; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
5. The proper Dismissal.

##### At Compline
After **"It is truly right"** (*Достойно есть*), Kontakion of the Image; **"Glory... Both now..."**, Kontakion of the Dormition[^468].

##### At Matins
1. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Богъ Господь*), Troparion of the Image twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast.
2. After the first and second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Image; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
3. 2 Canons for 12: of the Dormition with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Image for 6; Katavasia of the Dormition, after each Ode. After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Dormition, and Sessional Hymn of the Image;

**"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Dormition; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Image; after the 9th Ode, Exaposteilarion of the Image; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
4. At the Praises, 4 Stichera of the Image; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
5. The Great Doxology, and after the Trisagion, Troparion of the Image; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
6. The Great Dismissal, proper.

##### At the Hours
At all [the Hours], Troparion of the Image; **"Glory..."**, of the Dormition. Kontakia: at the 1st and 6th, of the Image; at the 3rd and 9th, of the Dormition.

##### At the Divine Liturgy
1. Troparion of the Image and of the Dormition; **"Glory..."**, Kontakion of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
2. Prokimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Hymn only of the Image; Epistle and Gospel of the Image, then of the day.

#### 2. On Sunday

##### At Great Vespers
1. **"Blessed is the man"** (*Блаженъ мужъ*) as usual.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), 10 Stichera: 3 Resurrectional, 3 of the Dormition, and 4 of the Image; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, 1st Theotokion of the Tone.
3. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
4. At the conclusion: Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
5. The proper Dismissal.

##### At Compline
After **"It is truly right"** (*Достойно есть*), Kontakion of the Image; **"Glory... Both now..."**, Kontakion of the Dormition.

##### At Great Matins
1. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Богъ Господь*), Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
2. All the rest up to the Canon is Resurrectional as usual.
3. 3 Canons for 14: Resurrectional with the Heirmos for 4, and of the Dormition for 4, and of the Image for 6. Katavasia of the day. After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Dormition, and Sessional Hymn of the Image; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Dormition; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Image; after the 9th Ode, **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Святъ Господь Богъ нашъ*), then Resurrectional Exaposteilarion; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
4. At the Praises, 8 Stichera: 4 Resurrectional, and 4 of the Image, with their two verses; **"Glory..."**, Evangelical Sticheron; **"Both now..."**: **"Most blessed art Thou"** (*Преблагословенна*).
5. After the Great Doxology, one of the two usual Resurrectional Troparia.
6. The Dismissal, as at Vespers.

##### At the Hours
At all [the Hours], Resurrectional Troparion, then **"Glory..."** at the 1st and 6th also of the Image, at the 3rd and 9th of the Dormition. Kontakia: at the 1st, Resurrectional; at the 3rd, of the Image; at the 6th, of the Dormition; at the 9th, Resurrectional[^469].

##### At the Divine Liturgy
1. Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Image, and of the Dormition; Resurrectional Kontakion; **"Glory..."**, of the Image; **"Both now..."**, of the Dormition.
2. All the rest is first of Sunday, then of the Image.

---

---

### August 29

#### The Beheading of the Precious Head of the Holy John the Baptist

All according to the Typikon of June 24, here on p. 243; nevertheless, by order of the Synod of Lviv, it is to be transferred from the weekday to Sunday with All-Night Vigil. It is usually transferred to the following Sunday; only if the following Sunday happens to be September 1, then the Feast is transferred to the preceding Sunday, lest it meet with the other three services, namely the Resurrectional, of the Indiction, and of Simeon.[^470] The fast of the Beheading, however, we do not transfer to Sunday, but observe on the very day of the Feast.[^471]

### August 31

#### The Deposition of the Precious Sash of the Most Holy Mother of God

All according to the Typikon of the Deposition of the Robe, July 2, here on p. 345.

---

### Notes

[^242]: Thus is named the first day of the year, from the Latin verb *indicere*, to proclaim or decree, because on this day taxes were proclaimed throughout the entire Roman Empire. Nilles, *Calendarium*, September 1.
[^243]: Synaxis (*Σύναξις*), that is, a gathering of the people for the solemn veneration of the wonderworking Icon of the Mother of God, which during the assault of the Iconoclasts was cast by them into Lake Gazoura (or Zagoura), near the Monastery of Miasena, not far from the city of Melitene in Armenia, and after a long time was found undamaged. (Synaxarion of the Greek Menaion of this day and Nilles, September 1).
[^244]: The Greco-Venetian Menaion has the entire Matins great.
[^245]: Pochaiv Menaion and Moscow Typikon.
[^246]: Thus the Pochaiv Menaion and the Moscow Typikon; our Sluzhebnyky, however, conclude the Kontakia with the Troparion of the Synaxis.
[^247]: Thus the Pochaiv Menaion and Moscow Typikon; Chapter 1 of Mark, however, which is in the *Tserkovne Oko*, in agreement with the Common Greek Typikon, which does not have Resurrectional Kontakia, appoints only the Kontakion of the Indiction and to the Saint alternately, that is, at the 1st and 6th to the Indiction, at the 3rd and 9th to the Saint.
[^248]: According to the rubric of the Menaion (September 7 at the conclusion), the entire Service is taken also to the Saint; among us, however, to a Saint without a Polyeleos nothing is usually taken; nor do we have need to take the Epistle-Gospel of the row, there being already another, except in Cathedral Temples at chanted Liturgies.
[^249]: Rubric of the Menaion, September 12.
[^250]: Menaion, September 13.
[^251]: Menaion, September 7. According to the earlier Typikon, the entire Service also to the Saint.
[^252]: *Tserkovne Oko* and Menaion, September 8.
[^253]: Menaion, September 15.
[^254]: Menaion, September 12.
[^255]: This Dedication, in Greek *Ἐγκαίνια*, in Latin *Dedicatio*, or Consecration of the Temple of the Resurrection of Christ, erected in Jerusalem upon Golgotha, occurred under Emperor Constantine in the year 335.
[^256]: The *Tserkovne Oko* and our Manuscript Typikon do not have this first Kontakion of the Dedication; the Greco-Venetian Menaion places it after the 6th Ode; likewise, neither the Kontakion of the Cross nor the Kontakion of Cornelius do we find in all editions, but only in our Horologion and the Greek Propaganda edition; of the Cross also in the *Tserkovne Oko*; the ornate manuscript Typikon of the 17th Century in the Library of the Lviv University (1. H. 4), and our Manuscript Typikon and the Greco-Venetian Menaion, have neither Troparion nor Kontakion to the Saint.
[^257]: The *Tserkovne Oko* (in the Menologion, leaf 268) and from it the Moscow Typikon (leaf 14 verso) appoint at the Hours only the Troparion of the Dedication, and *Glory...* to the Forefeast; the Kontakia of the Dedication and of the Forefeast alternately; to the Saint, nothing. We consider that such an Order is good under the supposition that the Saint has neither Troparion nor Kontakion. In those editions, however, in which the Troparion and Kontakion to the Saint are found, there is no reason to omit them at the Hours.
[^258]: Thus we consider; for in the case of the Kontakion of the Forefeast there is no rubric, as this was appended later.
[^259]: Title XI, Part 4, b.
[^260]: More simply, according to the earlier Typikon, the Cross with basil is placed upon a tray. Present-day Greeks more often, in place of basil, place flowers under the Cross.
[^261]: According to the Slavonic Typika, also the Phelonion.
[^262]: According to our custom, upon the Cushion, that is to say, upon the Pillow.
[^263]: Menaion, September 14.
[^264]: According to the Greek Typikon and the Slavonic one of the Lviv University, nothing is said during this Bringing-forth of the Precious Cross, except for the Troparion: *Save, O Lord, Thy people*: *Ἀπέρχεται ὁ Ἱεреὺς καὶ ὁ Ἐκκλησιάρχης σὺν τῷ Κανδηλάπτῃ ἐν τῷ σκευоφυλακίῳ μετὰ λαμπάδων καὶ θυμιαтῶν, καὶ λαμβάνουσιν τὸ τίμιον ξύλον τοῦ Σταυροῦ, ψάλλοντες καὶ τὸ Τροπάριον Σῶσον Κύριε τὸν λαὸν σοῦ, καὶ φέρουσιν εἰς τὴν Ἐκκλησίαν, καὶ ἐπιτιθέασιν αὐτὸ ἐπάνω τῆς ἁγίας Τραπέζης, καὶ ἅπτει κηρὸς ἔμπροσθεν δι᾿ ὅλης τῆς νυκτός.* (September 14). The usual beginning and the other preparatory prayers, that is, the Trisagion with *Our Father*, which the later Slavonic Typika appoint, are longer than this entire Order; for this consists only of the single Troparion of the Cross, and by the addition of the Slavs, also of the Kontakion.
[^265]: The ancient Greek Typika mention nothing concerning a Deacon either at the Bringing-forth, or at the Exaltation, or at the Veneration of the Precious Cross, but only concerning the Priest alone. The Greek Typikon, in agreement with the Slavonic ones, commands the Priest to vest in all the priestly vestments: *ἀλάσσει ὁ Ἱεреὺς πᾶσαν τὴν ἱεраτικὴν στολὴν*. The Greek Triodion (Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross) and the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, in agreement with the Slavonic Typika, command the Priest to enter with the Cross from the Sanctuary into the Temple through the North Doors: *ἐκ τοῦ ἀριστεροῦ Βήματος*. The Greek Typika, except for the new Constantinopolitan one, and the Slavonic one of the Lviv University, command the Priest, having gone forth with the Cross from the Sanctuary into the Temple, to proceed even to the doors leading from the Temple into the Narthex, sometimes called royal, and to await there the end of the Doxology, and to exclaim there: *Wisdom! Stand upright!* and thus to advance to the Tetrapod, which is before the Iconostas. If, however, these doors also are called Royal, *βασιλικαὶ*, understand this according to the ancient appellation; for according to present practice we call Royal the middle doors of the Iconostas, which according to the ancient appellation are called Holy: *ἅγιαι θύραι*. (Chapter 96, Greek Typikon).
[^266]: According to the Slavonic Typika, that is to say, according to the Pochaiv Menaion, according to the Moscow Typikon and the *Tserkovne Oko*, the Deacon sings the entire Ektenia, the Priest saying nothing. The censer, however, which among us the Priest holds with the Cross, it were better that the Deacon held, censing, even as the new Constantinopolitan Typikon and the *Tserkovne Oko* have it, September 14.
[^267]: From what is said above, it is evident that among us, in agreement with the Typikon of the Lviv University, the turning corresponds to the east, north, west, south, and again to the east.
[^268]: The Typikon of the Triodion for the Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross commands the Priest before the veneration and kissing of the Precious Cross to open the theca (*θήκη*), that is, the reliquary in which the Wood of the Precious Cross is preserved. This, however, can be performed only where a particle of this Wood is possessed, as in Monasteries.
[^269]: The Greek Typikon prescribes before the kissing of the Cross 3 metanias, and after the kissing one; among us today, however, custom maintains the making of three prostrations both before and after the kissing of the Cross. The Slavonic Typika say nothing concerning this. — The Insistent Ektenia, moreover, which follows according to the Typikon after the Doxology, may be omitted, since this has already been performed at the Exaltation of the Cross. Likewise the Greek Typikon prescribes for this day: *While the Great Doxology is being chanted, the Priest vests in all the priestly vestments, and taking the censer and the Precious Cross upon his head on a diskos with branches of basil (among us, however, without basil), he goes forth through the left side of the Sanctuary, candles preceding him, and stands before the Tetrapod, facing east, and says in the hearing of all: Have mercy on us, O God. And we begin the first hundred of the Lord, have mercy, the Priest signing at the beginnings with the holy Cross. Then he bows his head as much as a span from the ground, and little by little rises upright until the completion of the hundred. And when he arrives at the 97th Lord, have mercy, the Ecclesiarches raises his voice, that is, chanting Lord, have mercy thrice, and the Priest, perceiving that the hundred is reaching its end, signs again thrice. Then turning to the south side, he says: Again we pray... and we begin the second hundred, and the Priest performs the second elevation, as written above. Then having thus turned towards the west, he says: Again we pray, and we begin the third hundred. And when it is completed, the Priest turns to the north side, and says: Again we pray... and the fourth elevation takes place. And thus the Priest turns towards the east, and says: Again we pray... and we begin the fifth hundred. And after the fifth elevation we chant the Kontakion: Thou Who wast lifted up. Then: Thy Cross do we worship, O Master, thrice. And while the brethren are chanting, the Priest deposits the precious Wood upon the Tetrapod, and we begin to venerate, both the Priest and the Superior, making also three metanias before the precious Cross, and then they embrace [it]. And after embracing [it] they make one metania; and then [bow] to the choirs on the right and on the left. Then the brethren enter two by two in order, and venerate and kiss likewise. And we chant also the Idiomel Stichera, as long as the brethren are venerating, in Tone 2: Come, all ye faithful. And after all have venerated, the Priest says: Wisdom, and the Dismissal* (Sept. 14).
[^270]: Thus our Menaia and the Moscow Typikon (Sept. 15). But if the Typika say nothing concerning the regular Epistle and Gospel of the day, nevertheless it will be possible to take the same in the second place, as at the Apodosis of the Feast, September 21, when this occurs on Sunday; but if not, then neither at the Apodosis of the Exaltation should the regular pericope be taken, so that there may be a certain agreement among the Typika.
[^271]: Menaion, Sept. 15.
[^272]: In the Eparchy of Lviv this Vigil was transferred to the Sunday occurring before this day, under the condition that on the very day of September 26 the service of the Saint is chanted as Non-Polyeleos with the Oktoechos, with the Stichera at *Lord, I have cried* on 6, and with the Great Doxology. *Vedomosti Yeparkh.* of Sept. 4, 1898, Part XVI, p. 149.
[^273]: Thus our Pochaiv Menaion, and the *Tserkovne Oko*, and the Moscow Typikon. But in our Services this celebration is transferred to the Sunday occurring before October 11, if the 11th day occurs on Tuesday, Wednesday, or Friday; as for example in this year 1899, in which the 11th day occurs on Wednesday, the celebration of these Holy Fathers was transferred to Sunday, October 8. For this reason, in such cases the Sunday of the Holy Fathers can occur also between October 8 and 14. The Greek Typika, however (e.g. the Greco-Venetian of 1884), appoint this celebration on the Sunday occurring between October 8 and 14; but if the 11th day occurs on Sunday, then on this very Sunday they celebrate the Holy Fathers.
[^274]: According to the Typikon of our Menaion, the Moscow Typikon, and the *Tserkovne Oko*, all first of the Earthquake, then of the Saint. Our Sluzhebniks, however, generally do not appoint the divine service of the Earthquake, but only of St. Demetrius; while the Greco-Venetian Menaion of 1884 appoints all of the Saint only, and only the Prokimenon and Alleluia of the Earthquake.
[^275]: Thus the Typika both Greek and Slavonic; but since these are taken from the Greek, which generally do not have Resurrectional Kontakia, while the Slavonic do have them, for this reason we think that for Slavs it is necessary to take also the Resurrectional Kontakion in alternation with the other two, that is, at the 1st Resurrectional; at the 3rd of the Earthquake; at the 6th of the Saint; at the 9th again Resurrectional.
[^276]: According to the Typikon of our Menaion, the Moscow Typikon, and the *Tserkovne Oko*: Troparion: Resurrectional, and of the Earthquake, and of the Saint. Kontakion: Resurrectional, *Glory...* of the Saint, *Now and forever...* of the Earthquake. All the rest of the Earthquake, then of the Saint; regular Epistle and Gospel of the day subjoined. The Greek Menaion of 1755 indicates nothing of the Earthquake.
[^277]: It would have been better to transfer it to November 21, so that with the change of the Calendar, there would be no confusion in the newly published Church Books. For already in this year 1900, the 31st day of October corresponds not to the 21st, but to the 13th of November.
[^278]: Some think, following the editor of the Peremyshl Typikon of 1870, p. 271, that neither on St. Michael nor on the Holy Prophet Elijah is it proper to chant *Blessed is the man*, and they take not a single Kathisma then, because this Kathisma, as the Editor says, is found only in the Pochaiv Anthologion (*Трефоло́гїй*), while in the most ancient Menaia and Anthologia it is not indicated; and he adduces as the reason for this that: "Elijah did not die, and Saint Michael, being bodiless, is not subject to death." These reasons, however, do not satisfy. For as regards the Typikon, not only the Pochaiv Anthologion of 1777 and the Pochaiv Menaion of the same year, but also the Lviv Anthologion of 1643, and the great Typikon entitled *Tserkovne Oko*, printed in Moscow in 1615, that is, more than a century and a half before the Pochaiv Anthologion, and a manuscript Anthologion of 1736 which we possess, and the Typikon of the All-Russian Church printed in Moscow in 1859 with the approval of its Directing Synod, appoint the Kathisma *Blessed is the man* on these two Feasts. But even if some Typika, such as the Moscow Menaion of 1782 and the Greco-Venetian of 1884, do not indicate *Blessed is the man*, it does not yet follow from this that not a single Kathisma is to be taken: just as on Non-Polyeleos Saints no Kathisma is indicated in the Menaion, but the regular one of the day is understood; so also here the regular Kathisma can be understood, as indeed three ancient manuscript Slavonic Typika appoint: one of the Lviv University, another of St. Onuphrius in Lviv, and the third of the Library of the Chapter of St. George; likewise also the *Tserkovne Oko* (for this appoints two rubrics: one with *Blessed is the man*, and the other with the regular Kathisma). The reason why these Typika appoint the regular Kathisma on these two Feasts may be that their service originally did not have a Vigil, nor even a Polyeleos, but gradually with time grew to a Vigil. Concerning Saint Elijah, the Pochaiv Menaion writes: "Be it known that in the printed Greek Menaia and manuscript Typika, likewise also in the Slavonic manuscript and printed Typika, the service of the Prophet Elijah is set as sixfold with the Oktoechos." And regarding St. Michael, the Slavonic Typika cited above appoint for him a service of Polyeleos rank, but not completely: for in part they interweave his service with the service of the Oktoechos, as with a Non-Polyeleos Saint.
[^279]: According to the well-known interpretation of the Sacred Congregation of Indulgences, in the customary form of words: *Pater et Ave*, the number, for example 7, or 5, placed only before *Pater*, refers likewise also to *Ave*. See, for example, the Decree of the Sacred Congregation of Indulgences of March 22, 1847, *Concerning the Pious Exercise of the Holy Betrothed Joseph*.
[^280]: We find the service of Saint Catherine of Polyeleos rank only in our Pochaiv Menaion and in the newer Greco-Venetian; in the rest, however, Non-Polyeleos. — In later Greek Typika we find it appointed on November 25 together with the Apodosis of the Feast of the Entrance of the Theotokos, but in the most ancient ones on the 24th, as today among the Greco-Slavs. For thus says the Greco-Venetian Menaion of 1615: *Σημείωσαι οὖν, ὅτι εὕρομεν ἔν τισι Τυπικοῖς τῶν νεωτέρων, ὅτι ἔν τισι τῶν πόλεων ψάλλεται ἡ ἀκολουθία τῆς ἁγίας μάρτυρος Αἰκατερίνης ἐν τῇ κέ τοῦ μηνός, ἐν ᾗ καὶ ἀποδίδοται ἡ ἑορτὴ. Ἐν ἑτέροις δὲ Τυπικοῖς παλαιοτέροις εὕρομεν αὐτὴν ἑορτάζεσθαι ἐν τῇ κδ τοῦ αὐτοῦ μηνὸς: ἐν δὲ τῇ κέ ψάλλομεν τὴν ἀκολουθίαν τοῦ ἐν ἁγίοις Κλήμεντος Πάπα Ρώμης, καὶ Πέτρου Ἀλεξανδρείας, ἐν ᾗ καὶ ἀποδίδοται ἡ ἑορτὴ τῶν Εἰσοδίων.* [Note therefore that we found in certain Typika of the moderns that in certain cities the service of the holy martyr Catherine is chanted on the 25th of the month, on which the feast also has its Apodosis. But in other older Typika we found that she is celebrated on the 24th of the same month; while on the 25th we chant the service of our fathers among the saints, Clement, Pope of Rome, and Peter of Alexandria, on which also the feast of the Entrance has its Apodosis.] This transfer from the 24th to the 25th day, in the opinion of the Greeks, which the new Greco-Venetian Menaion explains (November 24, p. 158), was made at the desire of the Monks of Mount Sinai, so that coinciding with the Apodosis of the Feast, the memory of the Saint might be honored all the more solemnly. For we know that the body of the Holy Great-Martyr was translated by angelic arrangement to Mount Sinai, and buried there.
[^281]: The superscript marker ¹) printed after *Пра́зд* on page 152 lacks its apparatus footnote text at the bottom of the page in the 1899 edition, the footnote space having been entirely occupied by the long runover of footnote [^280]. The reference clearly belongs to the omission or alternate distribution of the Troparia and Theotokion when one of the saints shares a common Troparion or when the service falls on various days, as Dolnytsky discusses in detail on the subsequent leaves (cf. p. 154, note 1).
[^282]: The Typikon is silent concerning the Hours; for this reason we present it ourselves, taking the Kontakia in alternation, after the pattern of the Typikon of September 1 occurring on Sunday.
[^283]: However, regarding the Troparia: if there is only a single Troparion for both, then the Troparion will be according to the rule of a single Saint; but if there is a proper one for each, then it will be according to the rule of two Saints.
[^284]: An ancient manuscript Menaion which we possess appoints the Stichera at the Praises only to Peter; while the manuscript Typikon appoints Stichera to Peter at the Praises, and afterwards the Aposticha of the Feast.
[^285]: The Moscow Typikon takes the Refrains of the Odes; but since today we restrict the [biblical] Odes to Great Lent, for this reason we say here the Refrain according to the title and name of the Saint, adding: *pray to God for us*. Likewise the *Tserkovne Oko* says: "and to the Saints we sing the refrain according to the name."
[^286]: Where there is only a single Troparion for both, there is their Troparion, then *Glory... Now and forever...* of the Feast.
[^287]: According to the ancient Typikon, the Troparion of the Saints is taken with *Glory...*, likewise also at the 1st and at the 6th Hour; among us today, however, only for Polyeleos [Saints].
[^288]: *Tserkovne Oko* and the Moscow Typikon.
[^289]: According to the Moscow Typikon, the Troparion and Kontakion also to the Saint. According to our manuscript Typikon: Troparion: Resurrectional and of the Feast; *Glory... Now and forever...* Kontakion of the Feast.
[^290]: Without Polyeleos and with the Oktoechos, as in previous Typika, or with Vigil, as in our Pochaiv Menaion and in the manuscript Anthologion (*Трефоло́гїй*) of 1736 which we possess.
[^291]: Pochaiv Anthologion (*Трефоло́гїй*) and Moscow Typikon, December 11.
[^292]: The superscript marker ¹) printed after *Бг҃оявле́нїя* on page 159 (Leaf p161) lacks its apparatus footnote text at the bottom of the page in the 1899 edition. The reference designates the five Menaion Sundays relating to the Nativity of Christ and Theophany enumerated by Dolnytsky on page 158: Forefathers, Fathers, Sunday after Nativity, Sunday before Enlightenment, and Sunday after Enlightenment.
[^293]: Our Sluzhebnik, in the Typikon before the 32nd Sunday. The Greeks likewise take the Sunday concerning Zacchaeus immediately before the Triodion, yet only in four cases, that is, if between the Sunday after the Enlightenment and the Triodion there be either one Sunday, or two, or three, or four; and if there be one Sunday, in it they take the Sunday which is the 32nd among the Slavs (the 15th of Luke among the Greeks) concerning Zacchaeus; if there be two Sundays, they take first the 29th Sunday (the 12th of Luke among the Greeks) concerning the Ten Lepers, then the 32nd concerning Zacchaeus; if there be three Sundays, they take the 29th, the 32nd, and the 17th of Matthew, which is concerning the Canaanite woman; and if four, they take the 29th, the 30th, the 31st concerning the Blind Man, and the 17th of Matthew. (Thus have the *Evangelistaria* of the Greeks; thus also Leo Allatius in Assemani, *Calend. Univ. Eccl.*, Tom. VI, p. 516).
[^294]: Our Sluzhebnik in the place cited above.
[^295]: Typikon before the 29th Sunday.
[^296]: Thus our Pochaiv Sluzhebniks, and the others which are reprinted from them, appoint six cases of Sundays between Theophany and the Triodion, according to the number of the six lines of the Boundary Keys, beginning from the last unto the first line, and from the 29th regular Sunday unto the 32nd (Peremyshl ed., pp. 226, 231, 236, 241).
[^297]: Pochaiv Sluzhebnik, and those derived from it, in the Typikon for January 7; likewise also the Moscow Typikon for January 7 commands to omit the surplus ones.
[^298]: In the Typikon of the Lviv University: "If the Superior wishes, we chant *Blessed is the man* and the Polyeleos."
[^299]: In the Typikon of the Lviv University: "At the Liturgy... Epistle and Gospel of the Feast and of the Forefathers; Communion Hymn of the Feast and of the Forefathers."
[^300]: Thus the *Tserkovne Oko* in the Markian Chapter 114; our Typika and the Russian ones appoint also the Resurrectional Kontakion; however, if at the Liturgy they do not take the Resurrectional Kontakion, then why do they take it here?
[^301]: According to the ancient Typikon, the service is taken also to the Saint, if he has one; the Epistle and Gospel first of the Saturday before the Nativity of Christ, while the regular reading according to the Slavonic Typika is taken under the pericope of the Saturday before the Nativity of Christ (Menaion, Dec. 18, and Moscow Typikon).
[^302]: Thus the Pochaiv Anthologion (*Трефоло́гїй*) on the Sunday of the Forefathers, fol. 5 verso, and on the Sunday of the Fathers, fol. 215; likewise also the Moscow Typikon, fols. 114 and 124. In the Greek Typika and in our manuscript Slavonic Typikon we do not find the Sessional Hymn of the Fathers.
[^303]: The regular pericope according to the Typikon of the Menaion is omitted; according to the Moscow Typikon likewise also on the Forefathers.
[^304]: The Typikon is silent here; we adopt it therefore from December 24, when it occurs on Sunday.
[^305]: Thus the Pochaiv Menaion and the Moscow Typikon. Regarding the Troparia, our Sluzhebniks do not take the Troparion of the Forefeast in the second place here, for thus they are taken on the Sunday of the Fathers before the Descent of the Holy Spirit; nevertheless there is no necessity to follow this, for the Troparion of the Fathers can be considered Dominical. Regarding the Kontakia, our Prayer Books omit the Kontakion of the Fathers, and appoint a special Kontakion for the Forefeast, in which the Holy Fathers are commemorated.
[^306]: The Table which is at the end of the new Sluzhebniks likewise erroneously appoints the Epistle and Gospel of the Eve, without regard to the Liturgy of the Eve on a Sunday, which is dedicated to the Holy Fathers, and for this reason their Epistle and Gospel ought not to be omitted.
[^307]: Menaion, Dec. 20 and 31, but by no means at the Apodosis of Theophany.
[^308]: Pochaiv Menaion, December 22.
[^309]: Menaion, December 20.
[^310]: *Let us sing unto the Lord*, which is at the end of the Psalter, the Typikon of the Menaion for December 20 commands to omit from December 20 unto January 14; today, however, we omit them not only here, but also throughout the entire year, except for the Great Forty Days (Great Lent).
[^311]: The Greek Typikon indeed is silent, but the Slavonic ones clearly say: “And Liturgy on that day does not occur.” (*Menaion*, Dec. 25).
[^312]: Synod of Lviv, Title IV, § I, no. 2; and Title VI, Chap. VI, no. 4.
[^313]: In the Patriarchal Church of Constantinople all the Hours are read together, *περὶ ά. ὥραν τῆς ἡμέρας* [around the 1st hour of the day], yet without the Typika (*Typikon* 1884). According to the Greek Typikon, the First Hour is joined with Matins, while the rest on the Eve of the Nativity of Christ are sung *περὶ ὥραν ϛ* [around the 6th hour], and on the Eve of Theophany *περὶ ὥραν ἕκτην* [around the sixth hour]; when, however, they are transferred back from the Eve unto Friday, they are sung *γενομένης ἐννάτης* [at the arrival of the ninth hour]. In the various Russian monasteries cited by the *Tserkovne Oko*, the first two Hours are sung at the second hour of the day, and the other two with the Typika at the fifth hour of the day; thus also the Lviv Typika, but the 1st and 3rd Hour at the first hour of the day; according to the Typikon of our Menaion and the Moscow Typikon all the Hours are joined together with the Typika, and they begin at the start of the 2nd Hour.
[^314]: Title IV, Chap. II, no. 15.
[^315]: The Typika of certain Russian monasteries, cited in the *Tserkovne Oko*, command the Deacon to say three litanies at each Hour: namely *In peace let us pray to the Lord* after the conclusion of the Tri-psalmody, the Little Litany before the Prokimenon, and *Let us complete* after the singing of the Gospel. For these the Greek length does not suffice, and by misplaced additions they disfigure the form of the Hours handed down to us from the beginning; the ancient Lviv Typika likewise append litanies.
[^316]: According to the Slavonic Typika, the Priest and Deacon are to stand before the analogion throughout the entire Office, the Priest vested in phelonion, and the Deacon in sticharion.
[^317]: In the general Slavonic Typika, *Come, let us worship* with the preceding 12 *Lord, have mercy*, and *Glory... Both now...* is not mentioned, but is in every way presupposed; for in the Greek ones it is explicitly appointed, as also in certain Slavonic ones.
[^318]: In the Slavonic Typika these Stichera are called Troparia, but in the Greek ones Idiomela (*Ἰδιόμελα*).
[^319]: Thus has our Pochaiv Menaion. In the Lviv Anthologion (*Трефоло́гїй*) of 1643, and in the manuscript Festal Menaion of 1736, the same Sticheron is sung once by the Canonarch with the Refrain *Glory...*, then “sweetly with compunction by both Choirs, the right side one verse, and the left side the same,” and after this at the end *Both now...* the whole Sticheron once. The Graeco-Venetian Menaion of 1755 has it as does ours, yet places *Glory...* at the reading of the Canonarch. Other Typika take even the third Sticheron only twice; however, the new Constantinopolitan Typikon and the new Graeco-Venetian Menaion appoint before it the Refrain *Glory... Both now...*, then the Sticheron read by the Canonarch *εὐλαβῶς καὶ μεγαλοφώνως* [devoutly and loudly] (*Menaion*: *μετὰ μέλους* [with melody]), and at the end the same Sticheron a second time by both Choirs together. The Russian Typika take this Sticheron the first time without a Refrain, read by the Canonarch or Deacon with the addition of the Polychronion to the Emperor (in Constantinople: *λέγει τὸ ἐγκώμιον τοῦ Πατριάρχου* [he says the encomium of the Patriarch]). The verse *We worship Thy Nativity*, sung thrice, according to the Typikon of the inner Russian monasteries is sung each time higher, and highest of all *Show unto us also Thy divine Theophanies*, as the *Tserkovne Oko* says.
[^320]: The Greek Typika say nothing concerning the vestments of the sacred ministers, nor concerning censing. Our Menaion and the Moscow Typikon appoint censing at the beginning of each Hour, only at the 9th Hour do they appoint censing before the Prokimenon.
[^321]: The ancient Lviv Prayer Book appoints the Festal Dismissal; however, since in all other cases Festal Dismissals begin from the Vespers of the Feast, on this account the *Tserkovne Oko* and the Pochaiv Menaion better prescribe the Dismissal of the day, with commemoration also, as we deem, of the Saint occurring on that day.
[^322]: According to our common Typika and the Russian ones, Vespers with the Liturgy of Basil on the Eve of the Nativity of Christ begins at the seventh hour of the day (one in the afternoon), while on the Eve of Theophany at the fifth hour of the day (eleven before noon according to present reckoning); according to other more ancient ones in both cases: *περὶ ὥραν δεκάτην τῆς ἡμέρας* [around the tenth hour of the day] (four in the afternoon). The All-Night Vigil according to the general Slavonic ones, among which is the *Tserkovne Oko*, is at the tenth hour of the night (that is, toward morning); according to other more ancient ones, on the Eve of the Nativity: *πεрὶ ὥραν πρώτην τῆς νυκτὸς* [around the first hour of the night], and of Theophany: *περὶ ὥραν δευτέραν τῆς νυκτὸς* [around the second hour of the night]. In the Patriarchate of Constantinople, Vespers begins immediately after the Royal 9th Hour.
[^323]: According to the Slavonic Typika and in the Greek Typikon, the Priest performs the Proskomide when *Lord, I have cried* begins.
[^324]: At the Liturgy with Vespers the Greek Menaion (Venice, 1755) and the new Constantinopolitan Typikon after *Blessed is the kingdom* immediately take *Come, let us worship*; but the ancient Greek Typikon of 1615 in place of *Blessed is the kingdom* has *Blessed is our God*, and before *Come, let us worship* the Trisagion with *Our Father*. The *Tserkovne Oko* and our Menaia and the Russian ones have all things as we have set forth in the text.
[^325]: In the Graeco-Venetian Menaion, five.
[^326]: The new Constantinopolitan Typikon of the Patriarchate takes only three Readings, and after the first and after the second Reading the proper Troparia with the Verses, concerning which we have spoken.
[^327]: The Greek Anagnostika and our Menaia command the Deacon to exclaim thus before *Blessed is our God* at Great Compline of the Nativity and Theophany.
[^328]: The superscript marker ¹) printed after *Блаже́нъ мꙋ́жъ* on page 192 (Leaf p194) lacks its apparatus footnote text at the bottom of the page in the 1899 edition. The reference indicates that the Kathisma *Blessed is the man* (its first stasis) is taken on Saturday evening when the Eve of the Nativity falls on Sunday, but is omitted when the Eve falls on other days of the week.
[^329]: Thus the *Tserkovne Oko* and our Typika and the Russian ones; the Greek Typika, however, and the Ostromir Gospel appoint the Gospel from Luke, pericope 5: *In those days there went out a decree from Caesar Augustus*, as do also our Horologia; that very same Gospel, namely, which is also at Vespers with the Liturgy of Basil, as occurs on the Eve of Theophany; and this is better, since it refers more closely to the Feast. Besides this, if the Eve occurs on Saturday, then at the Liturgy the Gospel for the Saturday before the Nativity will be from Luke, pericope 72, likewise concerning the mustard seed.
[^330]: Because the Gospel outside of the Liturgy it is not the custom for the Deacon to read, but for the Priest; as was pointed out also at the Royal Hours.
[^331]: The Slavonic Typika appoint the censing immediately after *Blessed is our God*, by the Priest vested in phelonion; according to our custom, however, the censing occurs before *Blessed is our God*, and this by the Priest only in epitrachelion, or by the Deacons in dalmatics, if there be any, as the Synod of Lviv has in Appendix 36.
[^332]: The Greek Horologion, which we possess, appoints this Verse at the end only once, and this by both Choirs together; others, by one Choir once, then by both together once. And it commands to sing it only in Great Lent, in Tone 8, while outside of Lent only to read it quickly and without singing: *χύρα καὶ ἄνευ μέλους* [in speech and without chant].
[^333]: To sing only the end of the Troparion after each of the following Verses, we find also in the Troparia sung with the Verses between the Readings of Vespers of the Nativity of Christ and Theophany.
[^334]: Synod of Lviv in Appendix 36.
[^335]: Likewise also the *Tserkovne Oko* in Chap. 24: “And from the Nativity of Christ unto the day of the Holy Theophany... eat cheese and eggs, and milk and meat, on Wednesday and Friday as on a single day; for this is called the 12 days.” And again: “In the twelve days after the Nativity of Christ we dispense from all things.”
[^336]: Thus: “May He Who was born in a cave... our God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, and of her holy Betrothed Joseph, whose Synaxis we brightly celebrate today, of the holy, glorious, and all-praiseworthy Apostles, and of the holy Hieromartyr Euthymius, Bishop of Sardis, whose memory we honor today, and of all the Saints...” and so forth.
[^337]: Thus the *Menaion*; the Troparia *Have mercy on us, O Lord* and the Litany *Have mercy on us, O God* the *Menaion* does not mention, because these are not appointed in the general editions of the Greek Horologia.
[^338]: Concerning this case the Typika are silent; but since, if the Synaxis of the Forerunner (January 7) occurs on the Saturday after Theophany, the Typika join his Synaxis to the Saturday after Theophany, therefore here also it ought to be thus.
[^339]: The Moscow Typikon joins also the regular reading (understand: if there be no omission); according to the ancient Typikon it would be proper to join also the reading to the Saint, if he has anything, nevertheless to a Saint without Polyeleos among us they do not customarily take it.
[^340]: Typikon of the Slavonic Menaia at the end of December 26, when the Nativity of Christ occurs on Sunday.
[^341]: In Mark's Chapter 23 of the *Tserkovne Oko* and in the Moscow Typikon, the regular Apostle and Gospel of the Sunday is under the pericopes; nevertheless it is understood, if there be no omission.
[^342]: The Greek Menaia and Typika provide a Typikon only for two cases of Saturdays and Sundays between the Nativity and Theophany, that is: 1: for two Saturdays and two Sundays; 2: for one Saturday and one Sunday, and for that single Saturday and Sunday they provide the following Typikon: *Εἰ δὲ τύχῃ ἓν Σάββατον καὶ μία Κυριακὴ, ἀναγινώσκεται τῷ μὲν Σαββάτῳ, Σαββάτῳ μετὰ τὴν Χριστοῦ γέννησιν, τῇ δὲ Κυριακῇ, Κυριακῇ πρὸ τῶν Φώτων* [If however there happen to be one Saturday and one Sunday, there is read on the Saturday, that of the Saturday after the Nativity of Christ, and on the Sunday, that of the Sunday before the Lights (Theophany)]. And this Typikon indeed suffices for the case of a single Saturday with a single Sunday, because then this Saturday occurs within the limit of December (the 31st), and the Sunday within the limit of January (the 1st); nevertheless it does not suffice for a single Saturday with two Sundays, nor for a single Sunday with two Saturdays, because then this Saturday occurs within the limit of January (the 1st), as in the 6th row, while the Sunday within the limit of December (the 30th), as in the 3rd row; and just as it is not customary, according to the Greek Typikon itself, to read the Apostle and Gospel of the Sunday after the Nativity beyond the limit of the Feast of the Nativity, in January, but it must be transferred back to December 26, *διὰ τὸ ἀρχὴν λαμβάνειν τὸν Ἰαννουάριον μῆνα, καὶ τὴν τῶν Χριστοῦ Γεννῶν ἑορτὴν ἀποδίδοσθαι* [because the month of January takes its beginning, and the feast of the Nativity of Christ is concluded (undergoes Apodosis)]: so it is not customary to read the Apostle and Gospel of the Sunday before Theophany within the limit of the Feast of the Nativity, or the Apostle and Gospel of the Saturday after the Nativity within the limit of January. On this account it is no wonder if the Slavs supplemented the missing Typika for themselves. Nevertheless the Typika of our Menaion and the Russian ones set forth on December 26 for the Saturdays and Sundays between the Nativity and Theophany, probably through the negligence of copyists, are in certain points poorly drafted, and require correction, in our opinion thus: The Typikon of the first case: *If the Nativity occurs on Sunday*, ought to conclude thus: “as appears in the present Chapter in the order, when the Circumcision and Basil occur on Sunday” (as in the following case). The Typikon of the third case: *If the Nativity is on Tuesday*, from the words: “And for the Sunday” even unto the end, ought to be: “And for the Sunday which is before Theophany, we read the Prokimenon, Apostle, Alleluia, and Gospel on January 1, as will be indicated in the order, when the Circumcision and Basil occur on Sunday.” The Typikon of the fifth case from the words: “and on the morrow, we read the Prokimenon, Apostle, Alleluia, and Gospel first of the Sunday before Theophany, then of the Saint” and the rest as there. For that which the Typikon commands to read in this case at Matins two Gospels, one of the Sunday, and the other regular, we do not know whence it has this, because except for a single regular Sunday Gospel at Matins we know none, for the Sunday before Theophany has nothing at Matins.
[^343]: Menaion, December 31.
[^344]: Likewise also in Russia the Official Church celebrates the beginning of the civil year, as Nikolsky writes in the *Posobie*, saying: “On January 1 among us (from 1700 to the 20th century) the beginning of the new year is celebrated.”
[^345]: The Moscow Typikon appoints the Aposticha to the Circumcision, even if the Temple were dedicated to Basil.
[^346]: Since the ancient Typika do not appoint a Vigil, they also do not indicate Troparia at the blessing of the loaves; and to sing the Troparion to the Saint twice, and *Glory... Both now...* to the Feast, as the Pochaiv Anthologion has, does not correspond to the pattern according to which Troparia are taken at the Vigil. — “If there is no Vigil, at Compline Kontakion to the Saint; *Glory... Both now...* of the Feast. At Midnight Office after the 1st Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast; after the 2nd, Kontakion of the Feast; the prayers *Remember, O Lord* we do not say, but 12 *Lord, have mercy*, and the Dismissal.” Thus the Slavonic Menaia.
[^347]: Thus have the later editions, namely our Pochaiv and the Russian ones; but the most ancient Greek ones, namely the *Euchologion* of Benedict, and of the Slavonic the Ostromir Gospel, the *Tserkovne Oko*, and manuscripts have the Prokimenon only to the Saint, and the Apostle and Gospel only to the Circumcision; the Prokimenon therefore of the Feast and the Apostle and Gospel to the Saint is a later addition of the Slavs. Yet today the time has arrived not for the addition of new prayers, but for the abbreviation of ancient ones. The ancient Typikon, however, has this: that at Matins the Gospel is taken to the Saint, and at the Liturgy to the Circumcision.
[^348]: Menaion, Instruction of December 26, Pochaiv and Moscow.
[^349]: Menaion, Pochaiv and Moscow, in the Instruction of December 26.
[^350]: In place of *All of creation rejoices in thee* our Sluzhebniks take the Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the Festal Canon.
[^351]: From the Typikon of the Menaion and of the *Tserkovne Oko*, which we gave on page 196 in the Note, it would be concluded that on the Eve of Theophany there is no fast; however, from another Typikon of the same Menaion and *Tserkovne Oko* appointed on the Eve of Theophany it follows that also on the Eve of Theophany there is a fast without alteration just as on the Eve of the Nativity of Christ, except for Saturday and Sunday, that is, with eating once and abstinence from dairy (*naklo*) and fish, and on Saturday and Sunday with eating twice with the same abstinence. How these agree with each other, we do not see.
[^352]: We sing these Troparia as Idiomela, as in Greek Typika they are called Ἰδιόμελα.
[^353]: The *Euchologion* of Benedict concludes this Ektenia with the exclamation *For Thou art merciful*; while the Greek *Euchologion* of Cardinal Julian in the Grottaferrata Monastery places this Ektenia in an abbreviated form.
[^354]: These are the words of the *Euchologion* of Benedict XIV: *Ἴστεον ὅτι ἡ παροῦσα εὐχὴ, ἤγουν τὸ Τριὰς ὑπερούσιε ἀναγινώσκεται μὲν παρά τισιν· ἐν δὲ τῇ μεγάλῃ Ἐκκλησίᾳ (of Constantinople), καὶ ἐν τῷ ἁγίῳ Ὄρει οὐ λέγεται· Σὺ δὲ εἰ βούλει ταύτην εἰπεῖν* [It should be known that the present prayer, namely *O Trinity transcendent in essence*, is indeed read among some; but in the Great Church (of Constantinople), and on the Holy Mountain it is not said; but if one wishes to recite it...], and the rest.
[^355]: We find nothing concerning the Trikerion in the Greek Typika, nor in the Typika of the Slavic Churches not in union, but only in our Ruthenian ones. It is however the custom of many of our churches to use three Trikiria, one for each immersion; but in the Typikon only one is mentioned, and in this case therefore not all three candles of the Trikerion are immersed together, but one at each of the three utterances of the words *Great art Thou, O Lord*, concerning which Kulczynski also writes: *Trinae candelae accensae immerguntur singulatim in aqua* (Specim. Eccl. Ruth. Pars II. ad 6. Jan.) [Three burning candles are immersed one by one in the water]. Likewise also concerning the immersion of the fingers and breathing upon the water, the Greek Typika are silent; the Russian Trebniks mention only the blessing of the water with the hand.
[^356]: The commemoration of the Most Holy Hierarch the Pope of Rome is placed in the Anthologion (*Trephologion*) of Pochaiv in the year 1777; the Synod of Lviv commands to do thus (Title III, part 6, near the end).
[^357]: The *Tserkovne Oko*, and our Menaia, and the Russian ones.
[^358]: In the Greek Typika we find the Troparion *When Thou wast baptized in the Jordan* sung only once, and that only by the Priest (*Euchol.* and *Men.*). In the *Tserkovne Oko* the Priest sings *When Thou wast baptized in the Jordan* twice, the singers likewise singing the same each time after the Priest (fol. 177v). In our Menaion and the Moscow Typikon the Priest sings the same with the Priests three times, then finally also the choirs. In the Typikon of the Lviv Cathedral (*Vsesvyatilishche*), the Priest sings once, and afterwards the choir three times during the sprinkling. Concerning the cruciform blessing of the water with the Cross the *Euchologion* makes no mention, but the Graeco-Venetian Menaion mentions it, saying: *εὐλογῶν τὰ ὕδατα σταυροειδῶς* [blessing the waters in the form of a cross].
[^359]: The Greek Typika command to sprinkle the church, and our Trebniks even the Holy Altar. The Slavic Menaia provide the following Typikon concerning the sprinkling of the Monks: “The Superior, having taken holy water upon a plate, and having turned his face toward the west, holding the Precious Cross in his left hand and basil in his right, sprinkles cruciformly to all sides. And first the Priests come, two by two, and kiss the Precious Cross, and he signs them cruciformly upon the face with the basil and holy water; in the same order also all the brethren. Then the Troparion is sung by all many times, until all the brethren are sanctified by the sprinkling of water and by sipping.” Similarly also the Patriarch of Constantinople signs the Hierarchs and Church Offikion-holders (*Ὀφφικιούχους*) with holy water, as the New Typikon of Constantinople has.
[^360]: Thus our Typika, and the Russian ones.
[^361]: Our Typika and the Russian ones.
[^362]: If there is no omission (*otstupka*), our Menaion commands to read the regular pericope under the pericope (*pod zachalo*), that is, in the second place together with the Sunday after Theophany, then of the Saint (according to the Typikon of the Afterfeast of the Nativity of the Most Holy Theotokos on the Sunday before the Exaltation).
[^363]: The Moscow Typikon takes also the Prokimenon of the Feast in the second place; however our Menaion omits it, because the Prokimenon of the Sunday after Theophany takes the place of the Prokimenon of the Feast. But the *Tserkovne Oko* takes the regular Apostle and Gospel under the chapter (*pod glavu*), understand if there is no omission. The Menaion takes also for the Saint whatever he has, according to the Typikon indicated above concerning the Synaxis of the Forerunner here on page 227; however, for a Saint without Polyeleos today among us they take nothing. Our Menaion and the Moscow Typikon and another Manuscript which we possess take the Communion Hymn first of the Feast, then *Praise the Lord*; however if on the Sunday after the Exaltation they appoint first *Praise the Lord* then of the Feast, then here also it ought to be thus. Thus it is done also at the Apodosis of the Transfiguration and the Dormition.
[^364]: Our Sluzhebnik on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.
[^365]: The Typikon of Constantinople joins the service of St. Theodosius with the service of the Sunday and the Feast; however, since in this case three Apostles and Gospels would collide, that is, of the Sunday after Theophany, of the Publican, and of the Saint, for this reason the Slavic Typika better transfer the service of Theodosius to another day. But the said Typikon of Constantinople takes at the Liturgy the Apostle only of the Sunday after Theophany, the Gospel only of the Triodion, while for the Saint it is completely omitted.
[^366]: Similarly also on the Sunday of the Publican with the Apodosis of Theophany, after the Kontakion of the Feast, the Kontakion of the Triodion is taken with *Glory... Both now...*, here on page 229.
[^367]: The *Tserkovne Oko* leaves it to the discretion of the Superior to celebrate a Vigil.
[^368]: For if even the Sunday sticheron yields to this Sticheron, how much more so the Sticheron of the Saints.
[^369]: In the general Greek Typikon there is no Sunday Kontakion.
[^370]: Neither the *Tserkovne Oko*, nor the Greek Menaion, nor the New Typikon of Constantinople, nor the Slavic manuscripts which we possess provide a Typikon of the Three Hierarchs for the days of Cheese-fare Week; from this it follows that the general Typikon placed between the text of their service was used without distinction also on the days of Cheese-fare Week. Likewise also on February 24, when it occurs in Cheese-fare Week, the Graeco-Venetian Menaion (1884) takes the entire service only of the Feast.
[^371]: Thus our Menaion and the Russian one; but the new Graeco-Venetian Menaion does not transfer the service of the Hierarchs to another day, but takes it on that same Wednesday or Friday with the Liturgy of Chrysostom.
[^372]: Likewise also the New Typikon of Constantinople transfers the Forefeast back to Friday; but the new Graeco-Venetian Menaion transfers the Office for the Departed to the Saturday of the Prodigal Son.
[^373]: Thus the Pochaiv Menaion and the Moscow Typikon for February 1, and the *Tserkovne Oko* (in Mark's Chapter 35); however, we do not know whence at the Praises at *Glory... Both now...* a Sticheron is to be taken for the Forefeast, since that which is at the Aposticha of Matins would inappropriately be repeated at the Praises; it is better that that which is at the Aposticha of Vespers be repeated.
[^374]: The Typika of our Menaion and of the Russian ones; however, better the Typikon of the Lviv University Cathedral takes for the Saint only two Troparia.
[^375]: The *Tserkovne Oko* sings *Blessed is the man*; our manuscript Typikon leaves it to discretion to sing either *Blessed is the man* or the Kathisma of the day; the Greek Triodion takes the Kathisma of the day, likewise also the Typikon of the Lviv University Cathedral.
[^376]: The Typika of our Menaia and the Russian ones appoint an Entrance; however, not in accordance with the Typikon of the Triodion of Cheese-fare Saturday, in which an Entrance is not appointed, nor is *Blessed is the man* sung. Rather could an Entrance be admitted if *Blessed is the man* is taken; however, even *Blessed is the man* in no way accords with a Saint not having a Polyeleos.
[^377]: Our Menaion, and the Russian ones, and the *Tserkovne Oko* (in Mark's Chapter 40) say here: The Reading of the Feast as if only one; for the Greek Menaion has: τὰ Ἀναγνώσματα [the Readings], and the Slavic manuscript Typikon which we possess likewise has: Readings of the Feast.
[^378]: The manuscript Typikon which we possess, and the *Tserkovne Oko* in Mark's Chapter 40.
[^379]: Thus we find in all Slavic Typika.
[^380]: On Cheese-fare Sunday, if an Afterfeast occurs, it coalesces into the Apodosis, because with Monday the Fast begins.
[^381]: Thus Mark's Chapter 43 in the *Tserkovne Oko*, and the Pochaiv Menaion, and the Moscow Typikon; however, if there is to be agreement in the Typika, we judge that here also there ought to be the Dogmatikon Theotokion of the 1st Tone, according to the general Typikon of an Afterfeast of the Lord and of the Mother of God, set forth at the end of the Pochaiv and Moscow Octoechos under the Direction concerning the Temple of the Resurrection of Christ; if however not, then also in the Afterfeasts of other Feasts there ought to be the Festal Sticheron, which we also deem to be more in agreement with the ancient Typika, in which neither in the Forefeast, nor in the Afterfeast, nor in the Apodoses of a Feast is a Dogmatikon appointed on Friday evening, but the Sticheron of the Feast without distinction of Saturday from the other days of the week. (Our manuscript Typikon Chapter 20, and Mark's Chapter 11 in the *Tserkovne Oko*), although the general Slavic Typika in the Forefeasts and Afterfeasts and in the Apodoses of a Feast appoint a Dogmatikon. Moscow Typikon, September 21, March 24, August 14; *Tserkovne Oko*, September 21, September 21; Pochaiv Menaion, September 21; Typikon of the Lviv University Cathedral, p. 87.
[^382]: On Meat-fare Sunday an Apodosis does not take place, but beforehand on Meat-fare Friday, because a Festal service does not accord with the service of the Dread Judgment.
[^383]: On Wednesday and Friday an Apodosis of the Meeting does not take place (says the Menaion); if therefore it occurs on Wednesday, its Apodosis is on Tuesday; if on Friday, its Apodosis is on Thursday. If however on Monday, its Apodosis is on Meat-fare Friday, as you will deduce from the 1st case of the Table of the Apodosis. Simply, our manuscript Typikon for February 2 in Mark's Chapter 17, and the *Tserkovne Oko* in Mark's Chapter 46 appoint only one Typikon for whatever day of Cheese-fare Week except Saturday, which we give for Cheese-fare Tuesday and Thursday on page 259, only appointing on Wednesday and Friday 3 great prostrations at the Ektenia *Have mercy on us, O God*, and at the conclusion of Vespers and Compline.
[^384]: Thus, for example, at the Apodosis of the Feast of the Entrance of the Most Holy Theotokos on Sunday.
[^385]: Our ancient manuscript Typikon and another of the Lviv University Cathedral appoint only one Typikon for whatever day of Cheese-fare Week and without *Blessed is the man* on Sunday evening. The new Graeco-Venetian Menaion, however, takes nothing of the Triodion in Cheese-fare Week, but only of the Forerunner.
