[^386]: Our general Typika and the Russian ones and the Typikon of the Lviv University Cathedral do not mention an Entrance; however, our manuscript Typikon mentions it, and if not even the days of the Forty Days exclude the Entrance of the Forerunner, how much less does Cheese-fare Week.
[^387]: Concerning the removal of the Phelonion, the Typika say nothing; however, since from the end of the Readings this Vespers takes on the character of a daily service, on this account from then on it is to be celebrated in daily vestments. This also has place at Matins, which up to the end of the Praises is of greater solemnity, but from the Doxology is of lesser solemnity.
[^388]: Our manuscript Typikon and another of the Lviv University Cathedral do not transfer the service of the Forerunner to another day, but sing it with the Triodion, just as on the fast days of the other weeks. Thus also the *Tserkovne Oko* for February 24; Mark's Chapter 61, however, transfers it to Cheese-fare Sunday only from Monday.
[^389]: Our manuscript Typikon places the second Reading of the Triodion with its Prokimenon after the third Reading of the Forerunner.
[^390]: Only here do our Typika and the Russian ones omit the Entrance, whereas on Cheese-fare Saturday they take it; concerning which discrepancy we spoke in Note 2 on page 259.
[^391]: The Pochaiv and Russian Menaia.
[^392]: Since the daily Gospel of this Saturday contains something concerning the Fast, on this account our Typika and the Russian ones take here the Apostle and Gospel of the day first, and of Theodore under one pericope, then of the Forerunner; whereas on Cheese-fare Saturday they take first of the Forerunner, then to the Fathers, and of the day under one pericope. Our manuscript Typikon takes only of the day and of the Forerunner; for in the Greek Typikon no more than two Apostles and Gospels are ever taken at the Divine Liturgy, nor is one read under the pericope of another.
[^393]: The general Typika conclude the Troparia with the Sunday Theotokion; however, the *Tserkovne Oko* (in the 1st Sunday of the Fast) leaves it aside, and concludes with the Icon, because that is of the Lord.
[^394]: According to the Typikon of the Menaion for February 24, when this Feast occurs on one of the fast days of the Forty Days, Vespers with the Presanctified ought to be celebrated both on the Eve of the Feast and on the day of the Feast itself; on the Eve with the Readings, and on the Feast itself with the Apostle and Gospel of the Forerunner. However, among us now the custom is maintained to celebrate the Service of the Presanctified only on Wednesdays and Fridays, and on the other three fast days the Liturgy of Chrysostom, after which in Cathedral Churches the three last Hours with the Typika are sung as well, and Vespers at the 11th hour. And where on Monday, Tuesday, and Thursday the Liturgy of Chrysostom is not used, nor is there a Liturgy of the Presanctified, then on the Eve of the Feast Vespers takes place according to the Typikon previously indicated; on the day of the Feast itself, according to the Direction of the *Tserkovne Oko* (in Mark's Chapter 62) and of the manuscript Typikon which we possess (February 24), there must be a Liturgy of the Presanctified, even if it is neither Wednesday nor Friday, and at it after *Let my prayer be set forth* the Prokimenon, Alleluia, and Apostle-Gospel of the Forerunner are to be taken, and the Communion Hymn *Taste and see* and of the Forerunner. If however out of necessity a Liturgy cannot be celebrated, then we deem that after the Readings of the Forerunner there ought to be the Prokimenon and Apostle-Gospel of the Forerunner, just as the Typikon has concerning the Annunciation.
[^395]: Thus the Moscow Typikon; although according to the Slavic Typikon it takes the Temple as well.
[^396]: The Pochaiv and Russian Menaia.
[^397]: [In the original printed edition, this note is marked 2) in the apparatus while note 1) is placed above it; the texts are inverted by the typesetter.] The Manuscript Typikon and the *Tserkovne Oko* in Mark's Chapter 78 sing the service of the 40 Martyrs with the service of Akathist Saturday; likewise also the Typikon of Constantinople and the new Graeco-Venetian Menaion.
[^398]: [In the original printed edition marked 1).] Neither in the Greek Books, nor in our Slavic manuscript Typikon, do we find different Typika for this Forefeast, but only one for the fast days in the body of the text; the *Tserkovne Oko* however adds to this single one yet another abbreviated one for Akathist Saturday, and another for Lazarus Saturday.
[^399]: This Forefeast has this proper feature, that in it no service to the Saint in the Menaion is appointed.
[^400]: The *Tserkovne Oko* takes also the Canon to the Temple of the Saint for 4, and of the Octoechos for 4.
[^401]: (On Friday: after the 5th, 8th, and 9th).
[^402]: For thus the Greek Triodion and our Pochaiv one have for this Wednesday; we deem, however, that the Trinity Exaposteilarion ought to be said once and without "Glory... Both now..."; for "Glory..." will be the Exaposteilarion of the Cross, and "Both now...", its Stavrotheotokion. The *Tserkovne Oko* takes only the Trinity Exaposteilarion, while the Moscow Typikon takes only the Exaposteilarion of Wednesday of the Cross-veneration, then "Glory... Both now...", its Stavrotheotokion.
[^403]: These are consequences of the usual Slavic prolixity. We deem it better in this case to leave aside the Stichera at the Praises; for neither in the Greek nor in other editions do we find such a Typikon, but only in the Russian and Pochaiv ones, and those derived from them.
[^404]: The Typikon of the Annunciation on Friday of the 5th Week of the Fast.
[^405]: The Typika mention the Troparia only at "God is the Lord"; we, however, in accordance with the Typikon at "God is the Lord," have appointed the Troparia and Kontakia throughout the entire Divine Service. The *Tserkovne Oko*, since it does not appoint the Troparion of the Forefeast at "God is the Lord," but only of the Akathist, therefore according to it elsewhere also only the Troparion of the Akathist would be taken.
[^406]: The manuscript Typikon which we possess takes the second Reading of the Triodion after the completion of the Festal ones.
[^407]: This Feast has five Readings, of which the two relating more closely to this Feast: "Moses entered" [Exodus 3:1–8] and "The Lord created Me" [Proverbs 8:22–30], are read at Vespers on the morrow with the complete Divine Liturgy; but all 5 are read together at Vespers when the morning day will be Saturday or Sunday, which do not have the Divine Liturgy with Vespers.
[^408]: Our manuscript Typikon takes the second Reading of the Triodion with its Prokimenon after the completion of the Readings of the Feast.
[^409]: According to the Typikon of our Menaion and the Russian ones.
[^410]: Our manuscript Typikon adds also 3 prostrations at the final Trisagion; whereas the Typika of our Menaion and the Russian ones, and the *Tserkovne Oko*, appoint 3 prostrations also at "Glory to God in the highest," and after the Trisagion, and again after the final Trisagion.
[^411]: Our manuscript Typikon takes the Resurrectional ones, as usual (Mark's Chapter 32).
[^412]: Thus our Typika and the Russian ones; however, we do not know why one should depart from the general rule and not take the Resurrectional Stichera, as our manuscript Typikon has (Mark's Chapter 31).
[^413]: The *Tserkovne Oko* sings it on Thursday of the Great Canon.
[^414]: Our Typika and the Russian ones appoint 3 prostrations at "Blessed be the name [of the Lord]" and at the Dismissal; our manuscript one, however, at "Now the powers [of heaven]" and at "Blessed be the name [of the Lord]"; we, however, omit them.
[^415]: The Typikon is silent here concerning the Troparion and Kontakion; however, it is done thus in the case of Lazarus Saturday.
[^416]: Since here there are 4 Kathismata, that is, 3 regular ones and the Polyeleos, it is asked which Ektenias ought to be said here; for the general Typikon has only that. Furthermore, since the Feast has three Sessional Hymns, but here only two are appointed, which two of the three are to be sung here? Concerning the Ektenias: since our Typikon and the Russian ones mention an Ektenia after the 3rd Kathisma, it follows thence that after the 1st and 2nd Kathismata there are no Ektenias, which the Pochaiv Triodion also excludes after the 1st Kathisma before the Sessional Hymn of the Triodion for March 25; and under such a supposition two Little Ektenias ought to be said: one before the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, with the exclamation: "For Holy art Thou," and the other before the Sessional Hymn of the 3rd Kathisma, with the exclamation: "For Thou art good," or "For Thine is the dominion," according to preference. However, since not saying Little Ektenias at Matins on weekdays is found only in the Russian and Romanian Typika, but is not observed among us, on this account we deem that here only after the 1st Kathisma an Ektenia is not to be said, but after the 2nd it is to be said with the exclamation: "For Thine is the dominion," and after the 3rd with the exclamation: "For Thou art good." Of the two Sessional Hymns of the Feast, let the one after the Polyeleos be taken, and another from the first two, according to preference.
[^417]: The Pochaiv and Russian Typika appoint here a Dismissal, but the others by no means.
[^418]: The Pochaiv Typikon of 1780 adds: In the Temple of the Annunciation, the Refrain and Heirmos of the Feast.
[^419]: The *Tserkovne Oko*, Chapter 96, and the manuscript [Typikon], Mark's Chapter 44.
[^420]: Even though the Typikon is silent concerning this. For the rest, according to the Greek Triodion and others, except the Pochaiv one (which says concerning Compline: "if it takes place"), there is to be no Compline.
[^421]: Even though the Typikon is silent concerning this.
[^422]: The Typikon has also 3 prostrations at "Glory to God in the highest," and after the Trisagion, and at the final Trisagion; however, small ones [bows].
[^423]: Even though the Menaion is silent here, nevertheless it has it thus on Friday of the 3rd Week of the Fast.
[^424]: We deem [it is said] on Friday, and not on Thursday as the *Tserkovne Oko* has at the end of Mark's Chapter 74, because on Thursday there is Great Compline with an All-Night Vigil, without a Canon. It will be better in this case to omit the Tetraode altogether, as this rule is a superfluous invention of the Slavs.
[^425]: Concerning the Katavasia in the Odes where the Triode does not enter, the Typika are silent. Even if according to the general rule of the Apodosis of Feasts the Katavasia ought to be of the Feast, and that after every Ode, nevertheless we deem that the Katavasia of the Feast in this case has no greater force than during the Afterfeast, because the Festal Service in this Apodosis has little. We deem therefore that the Katavasia in this case is to be only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th Odes, and that is the Heirmos of the last Canon, that is, after the 3rd, 8th, and 9th the Heirmos of the Triode, and after the 6th of the Cross.
[^426]: According to our Typika and the Russian ones, the morning Tetraode is sung here.
[^427]: Concerning the Troparia here at Compline, and afterwards at the Divine Liturgy, the Typika say nothing; nevertheless, since at the Hours they appoint only those of the Feast of the Annunciation, so also here it will suffice to take only those of the Feast, for both the Akathist Service and that of the Feast are concerning the Annunciation.
[^428]: We have extracted the Typikon from Chapter 74, Mark's Chapter of the *Tserkovne Oko*, because our Menaion and the Russian ones do not appoint in this case a Typikon with an All-Night Vigil.
[^429]: *Tserkovne Oko*, in Mark's Chapter 105.
[^430]: Thus we deem, for the Typikon is silent.
[^431]: Our Typikon and the Russian leave it to choice to sing at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*) Stichera either of the Feast or of the Saint; but since this Feast does not have Small Vespers, nor Stichera at Small Vespers, while the Saint has them, therefore it will be better to sing only of the Saint.
[^432]: Our Typikon and the Russian take the Stichera of the Saint from Small Vespers; however, in those same Typika the Saint has his own proper Stichera at the Litiya, nor does he need to borrow from elsewhere.
[^433]: Pochaiv Menaion of 1873.
[^434]: The Typikon does not indicate the refrain to the Troparia of the Festal Canon; it will therefore be possible to sing the refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.
[^435]: The Typikon is silent concerning **"Having seen the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресеніе Христово*); however, if at Mid-Pentecost, and on the Saturdays of the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 5th Week in the case of a Saint with Polyeleos, and on all weekdays at Small Matins the Typikon appoints **"Having seen the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресеніе Христово*), then we deem that here also it is to be so, just as we have set down in the preceding case.
[^436]: The Venetian Menaion takes 3 of the Feast, and 3 of the Cross.
[^437]: In non-cathedral churches we take these only of the Cross.
[^438]: We omit the Canon of the Forefeast here, and sing it beforehand on Tuesday.
[^439]: The Constantinople Typikon takes first the Apostle, then the Fathers. On the Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner, May 25, the Slavonic Typika appoint the Stichera first of the Forerunner, then of the Fathers, however inconsistently with the Typikon of Matins, Hours, and Divine Liturgy, in which they appoint the Service first of the Fathers, then of the Forerunner, as for the Theologian.
[^440]: More consistently the *Tserkovne Oko* has here also, in place of the Theotokion, the Troparion of the Ascension, just as at Small Vespers.
[^441]: Thus the *Tserkovne Oko* has; our Typika and the Russian ones, however, in place of the Feast appoint at **"Both now..."** a Theotokion, but we do not know from where to take this Theotokion.
[^442]: The *Tserkovne Oko* takes that of the Ascension.
[^443]: At the 1st Hour the Moscow Typikon has the Resurrectional Kontakion, by no means the *Tserkovne Oko* (Mark's Chapter 114), nor our Menaion.
[^444]: The Troparion of the Ascension at Vespers, and here the Kontakion of the Ascension, the Graeco-Venetian Menaion also appoints.
[^445]: As we deem; for the Typikon is silent.
[^446]: The Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos can reach even unto July 2, in the case of the latest Paschal boundary, which is April 25. For from Pascha unto the Compassion 69 days are counted: namely, from Pascha unto Pentecost 50, from Pentecost to the Sunday of All Saints, and unto the second Sunday (of the Eucharist) 14, and from that second Sunday to the following Friday 5 days. If, therefore, April 25 is Pascha, the 69th day from Pascha will be July 2. If, then, the latest boundary of Pascha shifts backward by three days, the Compassion will fall on June 29; but if eight days backward, it will fall on June 24.
[^447]: As in the case of the Theologian on the Apodosis of Pascha.
[^448]: The Greek books do not appoint a Polyeleos, nor likewise do the Russian Menaia and Typika, nor our Manuscript Typikon which we possess; the *Tserkovne Oko*, however, appoints a Polyeleos; the Pochaiv Menaion also appoints an All-Night Vigil, and the Pochaiv Trefologion appoints an All-Night Vigil optionally; however, this is according to the abundant piety of the Slavs.
[^449]: As the new Typikon of Constantinople has.
[^450]: That the Deposition of the Precious Robe can occur on the Compassion, we showed in Note 1 on page 344.
[^451]: See Note 1 of November 8 on page 150 concerning the Kathisma **"Blessed is the man"** (*Блаженъ мужъ*), which some omit on the Feast of this Prophet, and on St. Michael.
[^452]: "The manuscript has nothing else from the Office of St. Anne except three Prosomia (different even themselves from those here), the two Doxastika for Vespers, and the Canon here. It also joins with these another Office of the holy women Olympias and Eupraxia." (Publisher of the Venetian Menaion of 1884).
[^453]: Titul. XVI.
[^454]: The above-cited Menaion, and the new Typikon of Constantinople.
[^455]: Pochaiv Trefologion.
[^456]: The Greek Menaion interprets this Procession thus: *Προεόρτια τοῦ τιμίου καὶ ζωοποιοῦ σταυροῦ, ἤτοι ἡ ἐξέλευσις αὐτοῦ ἐκ τοῦ βασιλικοῦ παλατίου εἰς τὴν πόλιν* [Forefeast of the Precious and Life-giving Cross, that is, its going forth from the imperial palace into the city]. More clearly Nilles: *Profecto hoc die processionis pretiosae ac vivificae crucis lignum sanctae crucis e palatio Imperatoris Constantinopolitano prolatum, in magnam Ecclesiam S. Sophiae translatum est, ut crastino die ibidem adoranda proponeretur, ad depellendos scilicet ingruentes per aestatem morbos, uti ex libris liturgicis notant Bollandiani in praetermissis ad 30. et 31. Julii (Calendar.)* [Truly on this day of the procession of the precious and life-giving Cross, the wood of the holy Cross was brought forth from the palace of the Emperor in Constantinople, and translated into the great Church of St. Sophia, so that on the following day it might be set forth there to be venerated, namely for dispelling the diseases that break out in the summer, as the Bollandists note from the liturgical books in their omissa for July 30 and 31 (Calendar)].
[^457]: As also on August 1 on Sunday.
[^458]: The *Tserkovne Oko* and our Manuscript Typikon appoint the Kontakion of the Cross before the Sessional Hymn of the Saint.
[^459]: Namely, of the portions of the true Cross on which Christ was crucified.
[^460]: Although the Greek Typika do not appoint the Lesser Blessing of Water on this day, nevertheless this custom is not proper to the Graeco-Slavs alone, but borrowed from the Greeks, among whom of old there was the custom of performing the blessing of water at the beginning of each month, and consequently on August 1, according to the testimony of Balsamon in the interpretation of Canon 65 of the Trullan Synod: *Haec statuente Canone*, he says, *novilunium quidem festum ante annos omnem memoriam superantes cessavit; ejus loco, Dei gratia, ut et ipse propitius reddatur, preces, ac Aquae lustrales in Ecclesia a populo fideli fiunt in uniuscujusque mensis principio* (Asseman. Calend. tom. I. part. I. Cap. XI. pag. 156).
[^461]: According to the Synod of Lviv; according to the ancient [custom], however, purple vestments.
[^462]: Thus have our Typika and the Russian ones.
[^463]: More precisely, after the Anaphora (*᾿Αντίδωρον* [Antidoron], the distribution of which was after **"Blessed be the name of the Lord"** [*Буди имя Господне*]), as our Manuscript Typikon, the *Tserkovne Oko*, the Pochaiv Menaion, and the Russian ones have.
[^464]: Pochaiv 1792, and 1771, and those after them.
[^465]: Although the Pochaiv Trebnik of 1771 also appoints these.
[^466]: In our small Typika and in the *Tserkovne Oko* at the end.

[^468]: Our Typika and the Russian ones appoint only the Kontakion of the Dormition; nevertheless, since on Sunday they appoint also that of the Image, therefore here also it is to be thus, lest we fall into inconsistency.
